## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12J-A: Blueprint Architecture Consistency Review

**What changed**: audit only — zero code, UI, route, repository, service, or migration touched. Cross-reviewed ADR-0003 through ADR-0009 (and every Blueprint companion document, `sprint-12c` through `sprint-12i`) against each other, the Educational Constitution, RAS, `learner-record-layer-decisions.md`, and the two shipped code artifacts (`lib/learnerBlueprint/` composition engine, `lib/core/identity.ts` resolver) — verified against actual file text this session, not recalled summaries. **Verdict: ⚠ Minor wording corrections required** — no structural, ownership, or computation conflict found anywhere. Five real findings: (1) ADR-0007/sprint-12e claim "exactly three" Educational Identity confidence bands but name only two (Emerging, Established) — a miscount, correctly implemented as two in the shipped type; (2) "Timeline" is overloaded across three distinct concepts (Growth Timeline the section, "Historical Timeline" the ADR-0008 lifecycle stage, and Evidence Trail/Trace/Timeline as three different names for one link-destination concept across ADR-0007 §11 and ADR-0009); (3) the "fourteen reserved future modules" claim is actually two different lists (13 items and 15 items, sharing only 9) both mislabeled "fourteen," compounded by a further miscount in ADR-0009's companion ("thirteen other" should be "twelve other") and a third, different illustrative subset in this sprint's own mission text; (4) a real architecture-vs-code gap, not an ADR contradiction — ADR-0007 §6/ADR-0008 §6's per-section Live/Snapshot/Historical freshness classification has no corresponding field in the shipped `BlueprintSection<T>` type (only a whole-Blueprint `metadata.freshness: 'live'|'partial'` aggregate exists, a different concept), which Sprint 12J's presentation work will need before it can render ADR-0009 §1's "never mistaken for Current" labeling requirement; (5) this sprint's own "Report Card → Snapshot → Current → Compass → Career" mission phrasing, read literally, doesn't match ADR-0009 §6/§9's actual frozen navigation (Report Card is a strict dead-end, navigation runs backward from Current, Compass/Career are parallel exits, not chain continuations) — flagged as a clarity gap worth one added sentence, not a contradiction. Everything else checked out clean and is recorded as passing, not assumed: Identity resolution confirmed single-path with zero bypass (grep-verified — only `composeBlueprint.ts` calls `resolveLegacyStudentId`); RAS compliance confirmed (zero duplicated calculations, zero Operating-Layer reads, re-verified by grep this session); ownership matrix confirmed unchanged and non-duplicated across all four later ADRs; every Constitution Article citation (I, II, III, V, VI, VIII, IX, X, XI) verified word-for-word against the actual Constitution document — zero misquotes. Eight concrete wording corrections listed, none altering any ADR's actual decision. Full detail in `docs/architecture/sprint-12j-blueprint-consistency-review.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: all of ADR-0003 through ADR-0009 and their companions, `docs/architecture/learner-record-layer-decisions.md`, `docs/architecture/reference-architecture-specification.md`, `docs/sprint-25-educational-constitution-and-migration-strategy.md`.

**ADR**: None — this is a review of existing ADRs, not a new decision. No ADR was amended; the eight recommended corrections are wording-only and can be applied to the existing documents without a new ADR.

**Tests added**: none — audit sprint, no code.

**Rollback considerations**: none — no code, schema, or route was touched. The eight recommended corrections (§10 of the review document) are not applied by this sprint; they remain open recommendations for whoever next touches the affected documents, explicitly including the one real code-level item (adding a per-section `freshness` field to `lib/learnerBlueprint/types.ts`) which is scoped as its own small future addition, not bundled into this audit.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12I: Blueprint Presentation Architecture (ADR-0009)

**What changed**: architecture and documentation only — no React page, component, PDF renderer, QR generator, API, repository, or service created or modified. Wrote **ADR-0009 — Blueprint Presentation Architecture** (`docs/architecture/adr-0009-blueprint-presentation-architecture.md`), the fifth Blueprint ADR, answering the one dimension ADR-0005/0006/0007/0008 left unresolved: how the same composed object appears across every real surface without becoming five different reports. Froze five presentation layers (Quick Overview / Educational Profile / Full Blueprint / Historical Snapshots / Evidence Trace, each nesting inside the one before it — never a fork), a fixed nine-step progressive-disclosure order (Identity → Academic Snapshot → Attendance → Growth → Compass → Career → Evidence → Timeline → Appendix, with each position's rationale stated), a navigation philosophy (single scrolling document with expand/collapse, QR as the only hard exit — tabs/card-grid/wizard alternatives explicitly rejected with reasons), a full Surface Matrix (shows/hides/summarizes/links/interactivity/print/offline per surface), the Report Card → Blueprint Snapshot → Current Blueprint one-directional navigation diagram, one-directional dead-end navigation rules for both the Learning Compass and Career Intelligence relationships (Blueprint exits into them, nothing routes back), and a full mobile decision table (Layers 1-2 always expanded, Layer 3 tap-to-expand cards, Layer 5 QR-only on every device with no exception). **Grounded in a real audit, not hypothetical surfaces**: found `app/(student)/blueprint` already exists as a live learner-facing "Blueprint" page built on the pre-Sprint-12G `lib/learnerIntelligence/` engine — a concrete migration target Sprint 12J will need to reconcile, not discover fresh; also re-flagged (not resolved, out of scope) the three duplicate parent-facing Career Intelligence routes and Academic Clinic's separate PDF pipeline as open reconciliation items. Full detail, the complete audit table, and every matrix in `docs/architecture/sprint-12i-blueprint-presentation-architecture.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0005-learner-blueprint-architecture.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0006-blueprint-educational-experience.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0007-blueprint-layout-and-experience.md` §14/§15/§16 (this ADR's direct starting point), `docs/architecture/adr-0008-blueprint-lifecycle-and-rendering.md` Part 6/7/8, `docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md`, `docs/architecture/reference-architecture-specification.md` §9/§10.7-10.8.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12H: Canonical Learner Identity Bridge

**What changed**: infrastructure/identity sprint only — no domain logic changed in Learning Compass, Career Intelligence, Projection, Evidence, Attendance, Report Cards, Academic Clinic, or Blueprint's composer internals. **The audit this sprint's mission required before any design work found the premise itself was half wrong**: Sprint 12G concluded no Core↔legacy identity bridge existed (a grep for `legacy_student_id`/`core_learner_id` found nothing), but a real bridge already existed under different names — `external_id` (already-provisioned schema on `students`/`teacher_classes`/`class_assessments`) plus `lib/core/academicBridge.ts` (Sprint 9F/9G, already live in production), including a pure read-only resolver `resolveLegacyStudentId(coreLearnerId)` already used by three existing wrapper functions. Sprint 12G's `composeBlueprint.ts`, built one sprint before this gap was properly understood, had unknowingly created a *second* identity-handling path (`BlueprintIdentifiers.legacyStudentId: string | null`, caller-supplied) instead of reusing the one that already existed — exactly the duplication this sprint was meant to prevent, already present in the codebase it started from. **Fix, not new infrastructure**: promoted `resolveLegacyStudentId` out of `lib/core/academicBridge.ts` (a module whose own header says "EXPLICITLY TEMPORARY... nothing outside it should ever import from it") into `lib/core/identity.ts` — the module whose own existing header already declares itself "meant to be the only place [identity resolution] happens going forward." `academicBridge.ts` now imports it back and re-exports it, so its three existing wrapper functions need zero changes. `lib/learnerBlueprint/composeBlueprint.ts` now calls the promoted resolver itself, once, internally — `BlueprintIdentifiers` no longer accepts a caller-supplied `legacyStudentId` at all, closing the defensive-null-check pattern the mission named. No new table, column, or synchronizer — the existing `external_id` mechanism and its lazy-creation path (`ensureBridgedLearner`, unchanged, still write-only, still never called by Blueprint) are untouched. **On the mission's explicit "challenge whether a bridge is needed" instruction**: recommended against attempting convergence this sprint — `docs/architecture/learning-intelligence-migration-strategy.md` already scopes full `students.id` retirement as a named 14-phase project (~30 FK'd tables, ~306 `studentId` references across 8 service directories, 57 API routes), explicitly rejects a *permanent* bridging adapter as an end-state, and is gated behind an unstarted Phase 0 — attempting any part of it here would necessarily touch the domain logic this sprint's own scope forbids. The existing temporary bridge, used correctly from one place, is the right interim state; full convergence remains the already-decided future direction, not a decision this sprint makes or accelerates. Full audit findings, rejected alternatives, and the retirement path (already named: Phases 0/5/11/13) are in `docs/architecture/sprint-12h-learner-identity-bridge.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/learner-record-layer-decisions.md` Decision 3 (the gap this sprint made structural, not new), `docs/architecture/learning-intelligence-migration-strategy.md` §3 (the existing rejection of a permanent bridge, and the existing 14-phase retirement plan), `docs/architecture/adr-0005-learner-blueprint-architecture.md`/`adr-0006`/`adr-0008` (Blueprint stays a consumer, never an identity resolver), ADR-0002 (Teacher identity, untouched, confirmed not duplicated by the promoted resolver).

**ADR**: None new — this sprint implements an already-existing decision (Decision 3's sanctioned interim state) more correctly; it does not overturn or amend it. The First Constitutional Law (one canonical identity per real-world entity) is satisfied by there now being exactly one *resolution path* between the two tables, not by there being one table — no ADR trigger condition (new canonical identity, changed ownership) was met.

**Tests added/updated**: `lib/learnerBlueprint/composeBlueprint.integration.test.ts` — the two Sprint 12G tests updated to the new `BlueprintIdentifiers` shape (no `legacyStudentId` field); **one new test** using `lib/core/academicBridge.ts`'s own `ensureBridgedClass`/`ensureBridgedLearner` (via a real teacher-onboarding fixture, matching `academicBridge.test.ts`'s existing pattern) to create a genuine bridge, then proving `composeBlueprint()`'s internal resolver call correctly transitions Academic Record/Learning Compass/Career from `unavailable` (no bridge) to `available` (bridge resolved, real Projection/Compass/Career calls attempted) — this closes the "known test-coverage limitation" Sprint 12G's own documentation flagged honestly at the time. `lib/core/academicBridge.test.ts` (pre-existing, 9 tests covering `ensureBridgedClass`/`ensureBridgedLearner`/ownership/idempotency/no-duplicate-teacher-identity) re-run unchanged to confirm the promotion altered no behavior. All tests pass. `tsc --noEmit` clean project-wide (not path-filtered). `eslint` clean on every touched file.

**Rollback considerations**: reversible. `lib/core/identity.ts`'s addition and `academicBridge.ts`'s re-export are additive/relocation-only — no call site outside these two files referenced the old location. `lib/learnerBlueprint/`'s `BlueprintIdentifiers` shape change is a breaking change to that type's public contract, but Blueprint has no consumer yet (Sprint 12I, Presentation Layer, hasn't started), so the blast radius is zero outside this sprint's own test files.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12G: Learner Blueprint Composition Engine

**What changed**: first code sprint in the Blueprint series (12A-12F were architecture-only). Implemented `lib/learnerBlueprint/` — the canonical Blueprint Composition Engine per ADR-0005/0006/0007/0008: `types.ts` (the one `LearnerBlueprint` type, `BlueprintSection<T>` wrapper carrying `status`/`owner`/`data`/`unavailableReason` so partial availability is explicit in the type system, not just convention), five I/O composers each reading exactly one owning domain's own function (`composeIdentity` ← `lib/core/learners`+`lib/core/school`; `composeAcademicRecord` ← `lib/projection/recompute.recomputeLearnerProjection`; `composeAttendance` ← `lib/core/attendance.getLearnerAttendanceHistory`; `composeLearningCompass` ← `lib/compass/session.getNextSubject` + a holiday-plan read following the existing precedent already used by `lib/learnerIntelligence/blueprint.ts`; `composeCareer` ← `lib/career/careerEngine.getMatchesForStudent`, top match only), three pure placeholders (`composeTeacherReflection`/`composeEducationalIdentity`/`composeGrowthTimeline`, all always `not_implemented` per explicit mission instruction — no algorithm, no AI, no classification invented), `composeParentSummary` (deterministic template over already-composed sections, no LLM, no generated paragraphs), `composeMetadata` (provenance stamp, no persistence, `freshness: 'live'|'partial'` computed from section statuses), `validation.ts` (`validateBlueprint()` — explicit `{valid, errors}`, never throws for expected partial states), and `composeBlueprint.ts` (the orchestrator every future consumer must call — concurrent composition, one domain failing never destroys the whole Blueprint). **The most significant discovery**: `docs/architecture/learner-record-layer-decisions.md` Decision 3 already documents, as a deliberately deferred and still-open decision, that Core's `learners.id` and the legacy `students.id` (used by Projection/Compass/Career/Learner Model) are two unbridged identity spaces with no linking column anywhere in the schema — confirmed by direct query against `lib/database.types.ts`. Every real Core-admitted learner today has no legacy student row, so Academic Record/Learning Compass/Career will show `unavailable` for every genuinely new learner until that named trigger resolves — made structural via `BlueprintIdentifiers.legacyStudentId: string | null` rather than papered over. **Two further gaps found and left honestly unresolved, not invented around**: no Teacher Reflection domain exists anywhere in the codebase (only Report Cards' reference-only `class_teacher_comment`), and neither Compass nor Career expose a canonical readiness/confidence *label* function — only raw numbers, which Educational Constitution Article XI forbids rendering directly — so `learningReadiness`/`futureReadiness` stay `null` rather than being computed inside Blueprint (which the mission explicitly forbade: "Do NOT calculate readiness... Do NOT calculate careers"). Confirmed by grep: zero raw Supabase `.from()` calls and zero reinvented capability/pathway calculations anywhere in `lib/learnerBlueprint/`. Full detail in `docs/architecture/sprint-12g-blueprint-composition-engine.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0005-learner-blueprint-architecture.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0006-blueprint-educational-experience.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0007-blueprint-layout-and-experience.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0008-blueprint-lifecycle-and-rendering.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md` §4, `docs/architecture/learner-record-layer-decisions.md` Decision 3 (the identity-bridge gap this sprint made structural), `docs/architecture/reference-architecture-specification.md` §9, `docs/sprint-25-educational-constitution-and-migration-strategy.md` Articles I, II, VI, IX, XI.

**ADR**: None new — implements ADR-0005 through ADR-0008 exactly as scoped; no new canonical-domain question opened. The identity-bridge gap surfaced is already governed by an existing decision (Decision 3), not a new one requiring its own ADR.

**Tests added**: `lib/learnerBlueprint/composeBlueprint.pure.test.ts` (17 DB-free tests: validation across identity-only/partial/full-status combinations, metadata freshness aggregation, parent-summary templating including missing-attendance/missing-academic-record degradation, all three placeholders, and the three legacy-identity short-circuits for missing-assessments/missing-compass/missing-career). `lib/learnerBlueprint/composeBlueprint.integration.test.ts` (2 tests against real synthetic Supabase data, following the Sprint 12B fixture pattern: a Core-only learner with real attendance records produces a partial-but-`validation.valid === true` Blueprint with Identity/Attendance available and Academic Record/Compass/Career explicitly unavailable; a nonexistent learner never throws, produces an explicit unavailable Identity and a failing validation result instead). All 19 pass. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both clean on `lib/learnerBlueprint/`. **Known test-coverage limitation, stated rather than hidden**: the `available` code path for Academic Record/Compass/Career could not be exercised end-to-end in this environment, since no real learner has a bridged `legacyStudentId` — verified by type-checking and code review only, a direct consequence of the Decision-3 gap this sprint surfaced, not an oversight.

**Rollback considerations**: fully reversible — `lib/learnerBlueprint/` is new, additive, and not yet imported by any route, UI, or existing pipeline; deleting the directory reverts this sprint completely with zero effect on any other code.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12F: Blueprint Lifecycle and Rendering Architecture (ADR-0008)

**What changed**: architecture and documentation only — no rendering engine, PDF, UI, API, repository, service, database change, AI implementation, QR generation, notification, or integration created or modified. Wrote **ADR-0008 — Blueprint Lifecycle & Rendering Architecture** (`docs/architecture/adr-0008-blueprint-lifecycle-and-rendering.md`), the fourth freeze in the Blueprint series and the one that answers *how* the Blueprint lives (ADR-0005/0006/0007 answered *what*/*meaning*/*layout*). Core decision: the Blueprint is never "generated" or "filed" — it is a continuously live composition over canonical-domain evidence, from Admission (Identity exists, everything else shows insufficient-evidence placeholders, never broken) through Continuous Updates (every domain event, never a Blueprint-side job) to Graduation/Alumni, with the same live composition object persisting rather than being recreated. Introduced **Blueprint Snapshot** as a genuinely new but non-owning artifact type — an immutable freeze of the Current Blueprint taken only at defined moments (report card publication, term end, graduation, never silently/scheduled) — and froze the permanent three-way distinction: Report Card (official term snapshot, immutable, Report-Cards-owned), Blueprint Snapshot (a frozen full-composition moment), Current Blueprint (always live) — each answering a different question, none competing as "the" canonical truth (reasoned explicitly from RAS §10.6's One Report Pipeline rule). Reduced the mission's illustrative state list down to five genuinely distinct states (Not Yet Active / Active-Building-Evidence / Active-Established / Alumni / Archived), explicitly rejecting five redundant ones (Initializing, Evidence Growing, Teacher Verified, Snapshot Published, Historical Snapshot) with stated reasons. Defined the rendering pipeline (Canonical Domains → Projection Engine → Blueprint Composition → Audience Filter → Paper/Digital Renderer → PDF/QR/Interactive Experiences) with Composition as the single point domain data enters — every later stage is a pure reshape, never a re-fetch. Made the Evidence→Meaning→Action / "what happened, why does it matter, what's next" pattern (first introduced informally in ADR-0006) a mandatory, universal per-section admission test for every current and future section. Extended ADR-0007's five audiences with School Leader (same single-learner view as Teacher — no cross-learner aggregate, since that's Analytics, already marked "No" for Blueprint in ADR-0005 §3) and Government (reserved, undecided). Verified all fourteen ADR-0007-reserved future modules fit the pipeline without redesign (companion document §5's compatibility table), flagging exactly one genuinely open lifecycle question left deliberately unresolved: whether/how an Alumni-state Blueprint reactivates if a future post-graduation domain (Employment Record, continuing certifications) begins producing evidence. Twelve permanent architectural invariants stated as binding on all future Blueprint work. Full diagrams (lifecycle, rendering flow, audience flow), the full educational event map (5 existing + 14 future domain events), and the future-compatibility analysis are in the companion document, `docs/architecture/sprint-12f-blueprint-lifecycle.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0005-learner-blueprint-architecture.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0006-blueprint-educational-experience.md`, `docs/architecture/adr-0007-blueprint-layout-and-experience.md` (all extended, not reinterpreted, per explicit mission constraint), `docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md` §5 (the "compute fresh, never cache" precedent generalized to every domain's event model), `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md`, `docs/architecture/reference-architecture-specification.md` §9/§10.6/§10.7/§10.8, `docs/sprint-25-educational-constitution-and-migration-strategy.md` Articles I, II, VI, IX, XI.

**ADR**: This entry *is* the ADR — `docs/architecture/adr-0008-blueprint-lifecycle-and-rendering.md`, status **DRAFT**, gating Sprint 12G (the first Blueprint implementation sprint).

**Tests added**: none — architecture/documentation sprint, no code.

**Rollback considerations**: none — no code, schema, or route was touched. Fully reversible by revising or rejecting the ADR. Per the mission's explicit Stop Condition, no implementation has begun. Four open items carried forward for whoever scopes Sprint 12G: the still-unresolved Sprint 12C worktree collision (`.claude/worktrees/agent-aedf323a0b5ed2eb3`), plus this sprint's own three flagged-open lifecycle questions (Alumni reactivation, Achievement-event ownership, Government audience rules, Archived-state retention policy).

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12E: Learner Blueprint Layout and Experience (ADR-0007)

**What changed**: architecture and documentation only — no code, UI, PDF, QR generation, AI implementation, or domain integration created or modified. Wrote **ADR-0007 — Learner Blueprint Layout and Experience** (`docs/architecture/adr-0007-blueprint-layout-and-experience.md`), the third and, per the mission, likely final freeze before implementation — after ADR-0005 (structure/ownership) and ADR-0006 (educational meaning/tone), this ADR fixes the artifact itself: cover page composition (school branding, learner identity, provenance stamp, QR verification, three one-line educational headlines — explicitly no scores or full sections on the cover), exact layout for all eight ADR-0005/0006 sections plus word/colour budgets (Attendance's colour rule: never colour alone as a risk signal), nine named QR destinations each single-owner-mapped (Learning Compass, Career Intelligence, Portfolio, Projects, Innovation, Evidence Trail, Digital Transcript, Holiday Programme, Future Skills — several reserved/inactive pending their owning domain's existence), school branding/provenance treatment (verification QR vs. digital signature vs. human signature blocks, all explicitly presentation not new data domains), one architecture rendered differently per curriculum (CBC Junior/Senior, 8-4-4, International, Cambridge/IB reserved) and per audience (a 9-row visibility matrix for Teacher/Parent/Learner/University/Employer — one document, filtered views, never recalculated per audience), printing rules (A4 portrait canonical, black-and-white-safe as a hard constraint since colour may never be the sole channel of meaning), device behaviour (same truth on every device, only layout density changes), fourteen reserved future-module slots (Behaviour, Innovation, Leadership, Projects, Portfolio, Community Service, Entrepreneurship, Competitions, Sports, Arts, Wellbeing, AI Skills, Digital Literacy), and a full five-audience open-to-close walkthrough tying every decision together. ## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12D: Learner Blueprint Educational Experience (ADR-0006)

**What changed**: architecture and documentation only — no code, table, migration, repository, service, route, or UI created or modified; no Blueprint, Academic Clinic, Learning Compass, Career Intelligence, or Attendance code touched. Wrote **ADR-0006 — Learner Blueprint Educational Experience** (`docs/architecture/adr-0006-blueprint-educational-experience.md`), extending ADR-0005's structural freeze with an educational-meaning freeze. Answered the Core Question ("what should a reader understand about this learner within five minutes") as: an evidence-grounded trajectory of who the learner is becoming, never a static score. Established three binding principles: (1) Blueprint always exists and is never "generated" — binding terminology (View/Open Blueprint, never "Generate Blueprint"); (2) Blueprint composes, never duplicates or recalculates another domain's output, even as a paraphrase; (3) paper stays concise, digital stays deep, enforced per-section via a purpose/owner/audience/paper-or-digital/freshness/QR/size table in the companion document (`docs/architecture/sprint-12d-blueprint-educational-experience.md`) covering all eight existing ADR-0005 sections plus two new architecture-only additions: **Educational Identity** (a single evidence-derived engagement-pattern label, e.g. "Persistent Builder" — explicitly not a personality test, must support an "insufficient evidence" state, never usable for ranking/comparison/high-stakes decisions, owning domain deliberately left undecided) and a fixed **Educational Philosophy** statement ("what the Learner Blueprint believes about learning"). Defined exactly what belongs in Blueprint's Learning Compass snapshot (focus/readiness/next action/holiday flag, never sessions/mastery maps/tutoring transcripts) and Career Intelligence snapshot (cluster/strengths/outlook/readiness, never the full report), reframed Attendance educationally (Trend/Health/Risk/Learning-Time-Lost/Support, still zero new attendance business logic per ADR-0004 §4), redesigned Teacher Reflection as five short structured subfields with an explicit AI-assists-drafting/teacher-remains-author boundary (Educational Constitution Articles VI, VIII), and defined per-audience reading order (Teacher/Parent/Learner/University/Employer each enter through a different section, same underlying data). Reserved eight future extension points (Behaviour, Portfolio, Projects, Competitions, Innovation, Community Service, Leadership, Wellbeing) with no schema or calculation decided. Flagged Educational Identity as this ADR's sharpest new risk (no existing canonical owner to defer to, real risk of drifting into an AI-generated personality label if implemented carelessly) and restated the still-unresolved worktree collision from Sprint 12C as unchanged and still blocking before any `lib/learnerIntelligence/` work begins.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0005-learner-blueprint-architecture.md` (extended, not amended), `docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md` §4, `docs/architecture/reference-architecture-specification.md` §9/§10.7/§10.8, `docs/sprint-25-educational-constitution-and-migration-strategy.md` Articles I, II, III, V, VI, VIII, X, XI.

**ADR**: This entry *is* the ADR — `docs/architecture/adr-0006-blueprint-educational-experience.md`, status **DRAFT**, gating the first Blueprint implementation sprint.

**Tests added**: none — architecture/documentation sprint, no code.

**Rollback considerations**: none — no code, schema, or route was touched. Fully reversible by revising or rejecting the ADR. ## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12C: Learner Blueprint Architecture Freeze (ADR-0005)

**What changed**: architecture and documentation only — no code, migration, repository, service, API, UI, or rename. (Note: this sprint reused the "12C" label already assigned earlier the same day to a separate, unrelated audit — `sprint-12c-academic-clinic-hardening.md`, "Academic Clinic Canonical Data Hardening" — the two are distinct pieces of work under a colliding number; this entry covers only the Blueprint architecture freeze.) Wrote **ADR-0005 — Learner Blueprint Architecture** (`docs/architecture/adr-0005-learner-blueprint-architecture.md`): defines the Learner Blueprint as the permanent longitudinal learner record, explicitly not a report card, AI chat, Compass session, Career Intelligence, Attendance, Behaviour, or Intelligence itself; specifies nine canonical sections (Identity, Academic Record, Attendance Summary, Learning Compass, Career Intelligence, Behaviour [reserved], Teacher Insights, Parent Summary, Learner Growth Timeline) each with exactly one owning domain and Blueprint owning none of them; a domain ownership matrix; a three-tier data-freshness classification (Live/Snapshot/Historical Snapshot) generalizing ADR-0004's derived-data discipline from Attendance to every Blueprint section; the QR-code strategy (short printed summary + QR to the live digital experience) as the enforcement mechanism for "one concise insight only" on Learning Compass/Career Intelligence; and the Report Card vs. Blueprint relationship (neither replaces the other). Freezes terminology: `lib/academicClinic/` remains the internal engine name, "Learner Blueprint" (served today by `lib/learnerIntelligence/`) is the public product name — no code renamed. **Findings the ADR states plainly rather than assumes away**: the codebase's `lib/academicClinic/` and `lib/learnerIntelligence/` are, per the same-day `sprint-12c-academic-clinic-hardening.md` audit, two independently-computed pipelines today (different capability/pathway computation, no shared report structure) — this ADR describes the target relationship, not current behavior, and does not schedule the convergence work. Also re-flagged an unresolved item from that audit: an uncommitted worktree (`.claude/worktrees/agent-aedf323a0b5ed2eb3`) already contains new `lib/learnerIntelligence/` files in the exact territory this ADR designates as Blueprint's home — needs reconciling before any sprint that touches that directory.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md` (the read-direction/no-summary-storage/consumer-computes-derived-values discipline this ADR generalizes), `docs/architecture/reference-architecture-specification.md` §9/§10.7/§10.8, `docs/architecture/sprint-12c-academic-clinic-hardening.md` (grounding fact-check for what the current code actually does), `docs/sprint-25-educational-constitution-and-migration-strategy.md` (Articles I, VI, IX, XI).

**ADR**: This entry *is* the ADR — `docs/architecture/adr-0005-learner-blueprint-architecture.md`, status **DRAFT**, gating every future Blueprint integration sprint (Parent Portal, Learning Compass, Career Intelligence, Behaviour, Evidence/Intelligence).

**Tests added**: none — architecture/documentation sprint, no code.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12B: Attendance → Report Card Integration

**What changed**: Report Cards now consume Attendance through the canonical service, per ADR-0004's first gated integration. Audit found `school_report_cards.days_present`/`days_absent` had exactly one, fully-built-but-uncalled write path (confirming Sprint 11A's original finding still held) and exactly one reader (`app/(parent)/report-card/page.tsx`, which already handles `null` gracefully and needed zero code change); found "Teacher report rendering" and "PDF generation/Printed report" don't exist as distinct surfaces in this codebase at all (nothing to wire attendance into). Added one new Attendance-owned function, `getAttendanceStatusCountsForClass` (`lib/core/attendance.ts`), returning raw per-learner status counts for a class/term — computed fresh, never stored — backed by one new bulk repository method, `listRecordsForSessions` (avoids the N+1 risk ADR-0004 §6 rule 6 anticipated). `generateReportCards` (`lib/core/report-cards.ts`) now calls it once per generation run and maps present+late→`days_present`, absent+excused→`days_absent` (Report Cards' own interpretation of Attendance's raw facts, per ADR-0004 §4) — `null`/`null` if a learner has no attendance data at all, never a fabricated zero. Historical-semantics decision (Phase 6): computed fresh at generation time, stored as an immutable snapshot, following the exact existing precedent `overall_score`/`overall_cbc_level`/`position_in_class` already set on this table — not a new rule invented for this sprint. `generateReportCards`/`runEndOfTerm` gained a leading `actorUserId` parameter (threaded from each route's existing `requireSchoolAdmin` call), since every Attendance read requires an authorized actor. **Mid-sprint, a real production gap was found and fixed**: the Attendance schema migration had never been applied to the real Supabase project (every prior Attendance sprint deliberately verified against an isolated container instead) — the moment Report Cards called Attendance at runtime, two regression tests failed with `attendance_sessions` missing from the real database. Asked the user explicitly; approved; applied the migration via `mcp__supabase__apply_migration`, verified via `list_tables`/`get_advisors` (zero new security advisories), re-ran the failing tests (now passing). Added a new end-to-end integration test (`lib/core/attendanceReportCardIntegration.test.ts`) proving the full pipeline against real synthetic data. Full detail, including the sequence diagram and ownership proof, in `docs/architecture/sprint-12b-attendance-reportcard-integration.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md` (every rule this sprint had to satisfy), `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md` §4/§13, `docs/architecture/sprint-12a-attendance-integration-architecture.md` (the roadmap naming this sprint).

**ADR**: None new — implements ADR-0004's first gated integration exactly as scoped; no new canonical-domain question opened.

**Tests added**: `lib/core/attendanceReportCardIntegration.test.ts` (new, 1 test, real synthetic data — proves the computed values and the null-not-zero rule). Three existing fixtures (`reportCardOwnership.security.test.ts`, `reportCardPublicationGuard.integration.test.ts`) needed one added line each (`addSchoolUser(..., 'school_admin')`) to satisfy a real precondition `generateReportCards` now has, matching what the real API route already requires in production. All 21 tests across 5 files pass against real data. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both clean project-wide.

**Rollback considerations**: the code changes are reversible (revert `lib/core/attendance.ts`, `lib/repositories/attendance.repository.ts`, `lib/core/report-cards.ts`, `lib/repositories/school.repository.ts`, `app/api/core/reports/route.ts`, `lib/core/endOfTerm.ts`, `app/api/core/school/end-of-term/route.ts`, and the test files). The migration application is **not** trivially reversible without a separate, explicit rollback decision — it now affects real production tables with real (if currently zero) data; rolling it back would need its own explicit approval, same as applying it did.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 12A: Attendance Integration Architecture (Audit + ADR)

**What changed**: architecture and documentation only — no code, migration, repository, service, API, or UI. Audited all 16 subsystems named in the mission (Report Cards, Evidence, Intelligence, Compass, Parent Portal, Teacher Dashboard, School Office, Academic Office, Analytics, Notifications, Learner Timeline, Career Intelligence, Behaviour, Promotion, Risk Detection, Academic Readiness) for their current/required relationship to Attendance, grounded in re-reading the actual current code rather than assumption. Key discovery: the Reference Architecture Specification already contains a reserved Attendance row (§3) anticipating "Evidence (one-way, when built)" as Attendance's only relationship to the rest of the platform, and an already-ratified Intelligence Standards section (§9) that already forbids any Intelligence module (`lib/projection/`, `lib/career/`, `lib/compass/`, `lib/academicClinic/`, `lib/adaptiveLearning/`) from reading an Operating-Layer table directly — meaning Attendance's Intelligence-integration rule didn't need inventing, only confirming and generalizing. Wrote **ADR-0004 — Attendance Integration Principles** (`docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md`): Attendance owns Attendance permanently (no domain may write it except itself, with one narrow precedented exception — Attendance may call Evidence's own existing write functions to produce evidence, never bypassing Evidence's own lifecycle rules); every read goes through `lib/core/attendance.ts`'s exported functions only; every derived value (e.g. Report Cards' `days_present`) is computed by the consumer, never stored by Attendance or cached across requests without a separate future ADR amendment; six integration rules future sprints must satisfy before writing code; five rejected alternatives (direct-read shortcuts, an Intelligence exception, DB-trigger-computed summaries, snapshot caching, a shared "Attendance Summary Service") each with a stated reason; six success criteria. Produced the full dependency/ownership graphs, a 16-row integration matrix, and a 9-risk table (circular dependencies, duplicate ownership/summaries, caching, identity, security, performance, repository/service-layer violations) with each risk's mitigation traced to a specific ADR-0004 section. Roadmap: 12B Report Cards (read-only), 12C Parent Portal (read-only, scoped), 12D Intelligence (via Evidence only — the real first deliverable is Attendance producing evidence, Projection/Timeline/Risk/Career Intelligence then consume it automatically through the existing unmodified pipeline), 12E Analytics (deferred, no demonstrated need yet), 12F Notifications (deferred, pending product/policy decisions). Full detail in `docs/architecture/sprint-12a-attendance-integration-architecture.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md` (§4 and §13 generalized outward by this ADR), `docs/architecture/adr/0002-canonical-teacher-identity.md` (identity reuse rule), `docs/architecture/reference-architecture-specification.md` §3 and §9 (both pre-existing and already dispositive for most of this ADR's conclusions).

**ADR**: This entry *is* the ADR — `docs/architecture/adr-0004-attendance-integration-principles.md`, status **DRAFT**, gating every Attendance integration sprint from 12B onward.

**Tests added**: none — architecture/documentation sprint, no code.

**Rollback considerations**: none — no code, schema, or route was touched. Fully reversible by revising or rejecting the ADR; nothing downstream depends on it yet since no integration sprint has started.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 11I: Attendance Operational Hardening & Production Readiness

**What changed**: a full 6-phase audit (every workflow, the lifecycle state machine, authorization for every named role, data integrity/DB constraints, UX completeness, and regression impact on every other domain), conducted by re-reading the actual current code rather than recalling prior sprints' claims. Two genuine defects were found and fixed, both minor and UI-only: (1) `app/teacher/attendance/[sessionId]/page.tsx` was missing the `membership === null` message every sibling Attendance page already had, leaving a user with no Core membership looking at a near-blank page; (2) `app/teacher/attendance/new/page.tsx`'s "Load Learners" button silently did nothing for a class with a null `academic_year_id` (a real, if rare, degenerate case the schema allows) — now shows an explicit error message. Every other audited item — session/record uniqueness, ownership chain, cross-school/cross-class isolation, FK delete behavior (cascade/protect/set-null), all ten Attendance route handlers' authorization, and every UI loading/empty/error/confirmation state — was confirmed already correct, with no schema, repository, or architectural change made or needed. Regression audit confirmed via `git status` diffed against the session's pre-Sprint-11A baseline: exactly one intentional file (`lib/repositories/index.ts`) was ever added to the modified list beyond what Attendance sprints were expected to touch; no Assessments/Reports/Learners/Teachers/Activation/Readiness file was ever touched. Full findings, the reconfirmed lifecycle/authorization/integrity tables, and the production-readiness verdict are in `docs/architecture/sprint-11i-attendance-production-readiness.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md` (every section re-checked against current code, not assumed), `docs/architecture/sprint-11b-attendance-schema.md` through `sprint-11h-attendance-operational-completion.md` (every prior sprint's claims re-verified, not taken on faith).

**ADR**: None needed — pure operational hardening of an already-approved, already-built domain; no schema change, no new canonical concept.

**Tests added**: none (not a stated deliverable). Verified live instead: 12 routes (4 Attendance teacher pages, the Administration page, and 7 regression-audit targets spanning Teacher Dashboard/School Office/Academic Office/End of Term/Reports/Classes) requested against the running dev server — all returned their expected auth-gated `307`, none 500'd. `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both clean project-wide (only the two pre-existing, Sprint-11-unrelated `TeacherBottomNav.tsx` warnings remain). Authorization and data-integrity claims were verified by tracing the actual code and the live Sprint 11B constraint-verification transcript, not by a new live multi-user/authenticated test run — that remains the one honestly-documented limitation carried through the whole Attendance arc.

**Rollback considerations**: reversible by reverting the two small UI fixes (`[sessionId]/page.tsx`, `new/page.tsx`) — no schema, repository, service, or API change was made this sprint, so there is nothing else to roll back.

**Verdict**: Attendance is now formally assessed as **production-ready as an independent operational domain** — the acceptance gate this sprint exists to establish before Sprint 12A begins any integration work.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 11H: Attendance Workflow Completion & Operational Validation

**What changed**: closed the two orchestration gaps Sprint 11G's own docs flagged, after a hand-audit of every teacher/admin Attendance flow found no other inconsistencies. **Phase 2 (completion state)**: added `getSessionCompletionState()` to `lib/core/attendance.ts` — a pure, computed-on-read function (`created`/`partially_marked`/`fully_marked`, from existing records + roster data, no new table/column) — exposed via a purely additive, opt-in `?includeCompletion=true` flag on the existing `GET /api/core/attendance/[id]` route (every existing caller's response is byte-for-byte unchanged without the flag). **Phase 3 (marked_by)**: `assertOwnershipChain` now returns `{ cls, schoolUser }` instead of just `cls`; `recordAttendance`/`bulkRecordAttendance` use the already-resolved `schoolUser.id` to set the session's `marked_by_teacher_id` immediately after a successful write — no new identity lookup, no API route change, no bypass of ownership validation (the two record routes are untouched). Deliberately did **not** clear `marked_by_teacher_id` on record deletion — it answers "who acted," a different question from Phase 2's "how much is done." **Phase 5 (admin accuracy)**: `app/teacher/core-office/attendance/page.tsx`'s per-class "Marked"/"Not marked" label (which, per Sprint 11G's own audit, was always "Not marked" since nothing set the field before this sprint) replaced with the genuine completion label, and "Completed/Pending Today" redefined as `fully_marked`/not, both scoped to only today's sessions + each class's one latest session (never the whole history) and showing "Checking…" rather than guessing while still loading. Teacher-facing Session Detail (`[sessionId]/page.tsx`) now shows the same completion badge, closing Phase 1's "reopen" gap (a teacher no longer has to count rows by hand). Phase 4 (edit lifecycle — reopen/edit/add-missing-learner/remove-record) was confirmed already correct by re-reading Sprint 11F's code; nothing needed changing. Full audit findings, the state-transition diagram, and the marked-by/completion-state rationale are in `docs/architecture/sprint-11h-attendance-operational-completion.md`.

Ownership-enforcement, editing, deleting, and session-lifecycle behavior were confirmed unchanged by re-reading the actual (untouched) functions, not by new tests. No live authenticated end-to-end run (real login, real marking, real completion transitions) was possible in this environment — same carried-forward limitation as every prior Attendance sprint.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 11E: Attendance API Layer

**What changed**: API routes only, per ADR-0003. Added `app/api/core/attendance/route.ts` (POST create session, GET list with `classId`/`from`+`to`/whole-school precedence — no new query shape), `app/api/core/attendance/[id]/route.ts` (GET/PATCH-metadata-only/DELETE for one session), and `app/api/core/attendance/[id]/records/route.ts` (GET list, POST single-or-bulk dispatched on whether `body.records` is an array, PATCH/DELETE taking `recordId` from body/query with a route-layer check that it belongs to the URL's session). Every route's only authorization gate is `requireSchoolStaff` — zero role arrays, zero `class_teacher_id` comparisons, zero inline permission checks anywhere in the API layer; "teacher only own class, admin across school" is enforced entirely inside Sprint 11D's `assertOwnershipChain`, never duplicated at the route. Status is deliberately typed `z.string()` in every Zod schema, never `z.enum(...)`, so the service's own `assertValidStatus` remains the single point of truth — validating it in the route would have duplicated the service. Grep-confirmed zero imports of `@/lib/repositories` or `@supabase/supabase-js` anywhere in the three files. Full detail, including a live smoke-test transcript, in `docs/architecture/sprint-11e-attendance-api.md`.

**Tests added**: none (not a stated deliverable). Confirmed structurally (via the 401-not-422 responses) that an invalid status string and a bulk `records` array both clear Zod and reach the auth gate, meaning neither is pre-rejected at the route layer — consistent with the "do not duplicate validation" design. Full round-trip verification of duplicate-session/invalid-status/bulk-validation/404 behavior *through* the API (i.e. past authentication) was not possible this sprint — same root-cause limitation carried from Sprint 11B/11D (no reachable database has Sprint 11B's migration applied, and this environment has no interactive login). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both clean.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 11D: Attendance Service Layer

**What changed**: service only, per ADR-0003. Added `lib/core/attendance.ts` — 14 functions (7 session, 7 record operations) composing `repos.attendance` (Sprint 11C) and existing Core identity helpers (`getSchoolUser`/`isSchoolAdmin` from `lib/core/school-users.ts`, `getClass` from `lib/core/classes.ts`, `listAcademicYears`/`listTerms` from `lib/core/school.ts`, `getClassRoster` from `lib/core/learners.ts`) — zero new identity-resolution code, zero direct Supabase calls (grep-confirmed). Registered `AttendanceRepository` in `lib/repositories/index.ts` (3 lines: import, barrel entry, re-export — alphabetically placed between `assessmentTypes` and `billing`). Business rules enforced: session uniqueness checked via `findSessionByUniqueKey` **before** insert (not caught as a DB-constraint exception); status validated against the exact 4-value set with no coercion/default; duplicate-learner rejected both within a submitted bulk batch and against already-persisted records; bulk membership validated against the session's class roster; the full School→Academic Year→Term→Class→Teacher ownership chain validated by one shared `assertOwnershipChain` function reused across every mutating/reading operation, with no inferred link (an unassigned class — `class_teacher_id` null — is admin-only, not "any teacher"). `bulkRecordAttendance` was deliberately reordered mid-sprint to run its pure input-shape checks (status, in-batch duplicates) before any DB round-trip — a genuine fail-fast improvement, and what made live verification of those two checks possible without a real backing database. Full detail, including the exact verification method and its one honest limitation, in `docs/architecture/sprint-11d-attendance-service.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md` (§4–§9, §13), `docs/architecture/sprint-11c-attendance-repository.md` (method signatures this service calls), `lib/core/permissions.ts`/`lib/core/academicBridge.ts` (error-hierarchy and ownership-check idioms reused, not duplicated).

**ADR**: None needed — implements ADR-0003 exactly; no new canonical-domain question opened. Deliberately did **not** reuse `lib/core/permissions.ts`'s `requireClassTeacher`, since that function queries the legacy `teacher_classes` table — reusing it would have bridged a Core domain (Attendance) through legacy identity, which ADR-0002/this sprint's mission both rule out; used `classes.class_teacher_id`/`school_users` instead, matching Sprint 11B's own identity choice.

**Tests added**: none (not a stated deliverable this sprint). Verified live instead: a temporary scratch script (`tsx --env-file=.env.local`, deleted after use, not committed) called the real exported `recordAttendance`/`bulkRecordAttendance` functions with fake ids and a bogus status, confirming (a) invalid status is rejected with the exact message before any DB call, (b) a duplicate learner within one bulk batch is rejected the same way, and (c) an empty bulk array short-circuits to `[]` with no DB call. Duplicate-session-across-the-database and full ownership-chain/roster validation were verified by code-review/call-order tracing only, not live execution — doing so needs Sprint 11B's migration actually applied to a reachable Supabase-compatible (PostgREST-fronted) database, which has not happened yet (the real project, deliberately not touched without separate approval; the local dev stack's `db` container, missing/broken since before this sprint, unrelated to this work). `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` both clean.

**Rollback considerations**: reversible by deleting `lib/core/attendance.ts` and reverting the 3-line `lib/repositories/index.ts` diff — nothing else imports either yet (no route/UI exists), so no other file would need to change.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 11B: Attendance Domain Schema Foundation

**What changed**: schema only, per ADR-0003 (now approved). Added `supabase/migrations/20260717_attendance_domain_schema.sql`, creating exactly two tables: `attendance_sessions` (one row per class/day/session_type — `school_id`/`academic_year_id`/`term_id`/`class_id` FKs, `marked_by_teacher_id` referencing `school_users(id)` — Core's own teacher-in-school identity, deliberately not `teachers(id)` per ADR-0002's Assessment-domain scoping — `UNIQUE(class_id, attendance_date, session_type)`) and `attendance_records` (one row per learner per session — `status` CHECK'd to `present`/`absent`/`late`/`excused`, `UNIQUE(attendance_session_id, learner_id)`). No summary/count/percentage column anywhere, per ADR-0003 §4 (Summary stays computed-on-read, never stored). RLS enabled on both, scoped to school-staff isolation only, mirroring `school_report_cards_staff`'s existing convention — finer-grained teacher-class authorization deliberately deferred to Sprint 11D's service layer. Delete rules chosen deliberately and verified: `school_id` CASCADEs (whole-tenant teardown), `term_id`/`class_id`/`academic_year_id` have no `ON DELETE` (protect — a class/term with attendance history cannot be deleted), `marked_by_teacher_id` is `SET NULL` (provenance only, never access control), `attendance_session_id`/`learner_id` on records CASCADE (owned-child convention, matching every other Core table's `learner_id`). No repository, service, route, UI, or business logic was written. Full detail, including live constraint-verification output, in `docs/architecture/sprint-11b-attendance-schema.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md` (implements §4–§9, §13 exactly), `docs/architecture/adr/0002-canonical-teacher-identity.md` (informed the `school_users` vs `teachers` FK choice — explicitly not re-litigated).

**ADR**: Implements ADR-0003 (now approved). No new ADR needed — no new canonical-domain question was opened beyond what ADR-0003 already decided.

**Tests added**: none (schema-only sprint, per mission — "do not write tests" was explicit). Verification instead performed live against an isolated, disposable Postgres 17 container (built from a stub schema reconstructed from `20260629_core_foundation.sql`'s real column definitions, since `schools`/`classes`/`teachers` predate this repo's migration history and aren't creatable from any file in it — a pre-existing condition, not introduced here). Confirmed live: migration applies cleanly; FK violations correctly rejected (nonexistent class); both UNIQUE constraints correctly reject duplicates; both CHECK constraints correctly reject invalid values; all three ON DELETE behaviors (CASCADE/protect/SET NULL) behave exactly as designed; `updated_at` trigger fires; rollback (`DROP TABLE` × 2) succeeds cleanly. The verification container was fully removed afterward — no real or shared Supabase project was touched. `tsc --noEmit` re-confirmed clean (no app code touched this sprint).

**Rollback considerations**: fully reversible — `DROP TABLE attendance_records; DROP TABLE attendance_sessions;` removes both tables along with their indexes, policies, and triggers automatically (verified live, see above). No existing table, route, or UI was altered, so no other rollback step is needed. Generated types (`lib/database.types.ts`) were deliberately **not** regenerated this sprint, since that requires applying the migration to the real linked Supabase project first — a separate, higher-stakes action not taken without explicit approval; flagged as an open follow-up in the sprint doc.

## 2026-07-17 — ADR-0003: Canonical Attendance Domain (design only)

**What changed**: no code — an Architecture Decision Record resolving Sprint 11A's audit into a design decision, ahead of implementation. Decides: Attendance becomes a new canonical domain (not a Report Card field, not Evidence, not Behaviour, not Intelligence output); three responsibilities — Attendance Session (stored, one row per class/day), Attendance Record (stored, one row per learner per session, append-only/supersede-not-edit), Attendance Summary (computed on read, never a second stored truth, mirroring `fetchClassTermStatuses()`'s existing pattern); ownership chain School→Year→Term→Class→Session→Record→Learner reusing every existing Core identity, no new identity or role; four canonical statuses (`present`/`absent`/`late`/`excused`, with `early_departure` deferred to an optional timestamp field rather than a fifth status); lifecycle mirrors the existing Assessment→Report Card pipeline; security reuses `requireSchoolMembership`/`requireSchoolAdmin`/`ADMIN_TIER_ROLES` exactly, with teacher ownership resolved via current class-teaching relationship, never via who marked a row (same rule as Evidence); 7 alternatives considered and rejected (single-table, JSON blob, on-learner, status-quo report-card-only, as-Evidence, as-Behaviour, no-session-concept), each with a stated reason; 8-sprint implementation roadmap (11B schema → 11C repository → 11D service → 11E teacher UI → 11F Academic Office integration → 11G report card integration → 11H Evidence integration → 11I parent notifications), each separately gated. Full detail in `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md`.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/sprint-11a-attendance-domain-foundation.md` (the audit this ADR resolves), `docs/architecture/academic-evidence-layer.md` and `learner-record-layer-decisions.md` (Evidence Lifecycle's supersede-not-edit precedent, reserved `EvidencePayload` attendance variant), `docs/architecture/adr/0002-canonical-teacher-identity.md` (this ADR explicitly defers to whichever table ADR-0002 settles as canonical for "who teaches this class," rather than re-litigating it).

**ADR**: This entry *is* the ADR — `docs/architecture/adr-0003-attendance-domain.md`, status **DRAFT**, satisfying the trigger condition flagged in Sprint 11A's audit ("introduces a new canonical domain").

**Tests added**: none — design document only, no code.

**Rollback considerations**: none — no code, schema, or route was touched. Fully reversible by simply not proceeding to Sprint 11B; the ADR can be revised or rejected without any implementation to unwind.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 11A: Attendance Domain Foundation (audit only)

**What changed**: no code — a full read-only repository audit for a proposed new Attendance domain. Findings: no attendance table or dedicated API/UI/repository/service exists anywhere; the only real touchpoint is `days_present`/`days_absent` columns on `school_report_cards` with a fully-built-but-uncalled write path (`updateReportCard()` / `/api/core/reports` `action:'update'`) and an always-empty parent-facing display row; the privacy policy (`app/(marketing)/legal/privacy/page.tsx:236`) claims EduNexus Core collects attendance data that no UI actually populates today — a copy/reality gap worth closing, not opening further. Two independent prior architecture docs (`academic-evidence-layer.md`, `learner-record-layer-decisions.md`) already recommended building attendance "on demand" via a generic Evidence-source shape rather than speculatively — this audit confirms that recommendation still holds. Produced a full domain model (Attendance Session/Record/Status, two-table recommendation), lifecycle map (mirrors the existing Assessment→Report Card pipeline), role inventory (reuses all six existing roles, no new role), integration audit (8 future integration points, none built), repository/security requirements, and an 8-commit implementation plan — none of it started. Full detail in `docs/architecture/sprint-11a-attendance-domain-foundation.md`.

**ADR**: **Needed before implementation, not yet written.** Introducing Attendance as a new canonical domain literally meets the Guardian mode's "introduces a new architectural layer or canonical domain" trigger condition. Recommended ADR scope: just the one open design question (one-table vs. two-table session/record shape) — no other part of the plan is architecturally contested.

**Tests added**: none — audit only, no code.

## 2026-07-17 — Sprint 10H Phase 2: Academic Office Workspace

**What changed**: pure UI composition, no new business logic. Added `app/teacher/core-office/academic/page.tsx`, a new "Academic Office" section inside the existing School Office workspace, organizing every academic-administration capability into one flow: Academic Structure (Years/Terms/Subjects/Classes, read-only status — no dedicated CRUD screens exist for these yet, stated plainly rather than fabricated) → Academic Operations (Assessment Progress linking to `/teacher/core-term/status`; Assessment Lock/End of Term/Report Generation/Report Publication grouped under one card linking once to `/teacher/core-term`, since all four live as buttons on that single existing screen) → Workflow Status (Completed/Needs Attention/Next Step, derived only from `getSchoolAcademicReadiness()` and `fetchClassTermStatuses()` — no new readiness calculation) → Future Modules (Promotion, Transfer, Graduation, Timetable, Departments, Attendance — clearly labelled "Planned future module," no buttons, no fake actions). Removed the "Classes"/"Assessments"/"End of Term" cards and the inline "Academic Structure" section from `app/teacher/core-office/page.tsx` (the data now lives only on the new page) and replaced them with a single "Academic Office" card, so every academic screen now has exactly one canonical entry: School Office → Academic Office → existing page. Extended `components/core/OperationalBreadcrumb.tsx` with an optional `parent` link (backward compatible) so `core-term` and `core-term/status` now show "School Office › Academic Office › <page>". The plain-teacher "End of Term" sidebar/bottom-nav entry (`/teacher/core-term`, visible to all teachers, not just admin-tier) was deliberately left untouched — it serves a different audience (a teacher managing their own class) than the admin-tier Academic Office rollup.

**Architectural documents referenced**: `docs/architecture/sprint-10h-academic-office-workspace.md` (Phase 1 inventory + Architecture Guardian/Phase B assessment governing this phase).

Full `next build` timed out in this environment before completing (large project) — not confirmed as a hard failure, flagged as a known limitation rather than claimed as verified.

## 2026-07-16 — Sprint 10E: Operational Activation, Phase 1 (Team, Admissions, Readiness, Activation Feedback)

**What changed**: closed the top four "Immediate" items from Sprint 10D's Part 7 priority matrix — the four highest-value gaps where already-built, already-tested backend had zero UI path. No new business logic was written; every mutation this sprint's screens perform calls an existing, unmodified Core function exactly as built in Sprints 9B–9E.

- **Team screen** (`app/teacher/core-team/page.tsx`, new): invite-by-email + pending/active roster, over `POST /api/core/teachers` (`action:'invite'`, Sprint 9C, unchanged) and a new admin-gated `GET ...&list=true` branch on the same route. The list branch calls a new aggregator, `listTeacherMemberships()` (`lib/core/teacherOnboarding.ts`), which composes `listSchoolUsers()` with two new batched repository reads (`findProfilesByUserIds`, `findAuthUsersByIds` — `lib/repositories/teacher.repository.ts`, both `.in()`-batched per CLAUDE.md, mirroring the existing `findUserIdsWithTeacherRecord`/`findAuthUserByEmail` patterns exactly, not a new query strategy). No email/token invitation system was built — an invite to an email with no EduNexus account surfaces the existing `no_account` status as-is, per Sprint 9C's documented scope boundary.
- **Admissions screen** (`app/teacher/core-admissions/page.tsx`, new): a single form for Admission → Guardian (optional) → Enrollment, over `POST /api/core/learners`'s `class_id` branch (`onboardLearner()`, Sprint 9D, unchanged). Every field maps 1:1 onto that route's existing `OnboardSchema`; this page adds no validation of its own.
- **Readiness/operational dashboard** (`app/teacher/core-readiness/page.tsx`, new): renders `getSchoolAcademicReadiness()` (`lib/core/academicActivation.ts`, Sprint 9E) exactly as computed — the function Sprint 10D found had zero route and zero caller. A new thin wrapper, `GET /api/core/academic-readiness` (auth + `requireSchoolAdmin` + one service call, RAS §2-compliant), exposes it. This page also links to Team, Admissions, and End of Term, serving as the "what still needs to happen before teaching starts" landing screen Sprint 10D's Part 6 asked for.
- **Activation feedback** (`app/admin/core-schools/new/page.tsx`): the `activation` result `POST /api/core/school` has always returned (Sprint 9C) was previously read from the response body and discarded, immediately redirecting to `/admin`. The page now shows a step-by-step activation summary (academic year/terms/grades/streams/classes/settings, each with its status) before the admin continues — closing Sprint 10D's specifically-cited finding.

**Known limitation, stated honestly, not worked around**: no code in this sprint introduces a new identity, write path, repository, or authorization check — every new read route reuses `requireSchoolAdmin`/`requireSchoolMembership` exactly as existing Core routes do. The one small extraction, `lib/core/adminTierRoles.ts` (the `['school_admin','headteacher','deputy_headteacher']` literal, previously duplicated once in `app/teacher/core-term/status/page.tsx`), removes a duplication rather than adding one; that page was updated to import it.

## 2026-07-16 — Sprint 10C: School Operational Readiness Audit (read-only)

**What changed**: nothing — this was a read-only architectural audit, no code/schema/migration touched. Full findings in `docs/architecture/sprint-10c-school-operational-readiness.md`. Method: three parallel research passes (screen inventory/navigation; role-by-role/daily-timeline; decision-flow/communication/workload), each independently re-verifying Sprint 10B's and Sprint 9A's prior findings against the current working tree — including the session's own uncommitted diff (`lib/core/{schoolActivation,teacherOnboarding,academicActivation,academicBridge,learnerOnboarding}.ts`, `app/api/core/teachers/`, and modified `app/api/core/{learners,school}` routes) — rather than assuming they still held.

**Headline finding**: since Sprint 10B, a real orchestration layer landed — `activateSchool()`, `inviteTeacher()`/`acceptTeacherInvitation()`, `onboardLearner()`, `getSchoolAcademicReadiness()` — collapsing what Sprint 9A found to be 6+ manual API calls across 5 route files into single, idempotent, tested operations. This is the first sprint in the series to find the Academic Office capability gap actually shrinking. But the shrinkage stopped at the API boundary in three of four cases: school activation is now genuinely reachable through a real, nav-linked screen (`app/admin/core-schools/new` → `POST /api/core/school` → `activateSchool()`, one request); teacher invitation and learner onboarding got the identical backend treatment but have zero `.tsx` callers — an academic office can now `curl` one orchestrated endpoint instead of three, but still cannot invite a teacher or admit-and-enroll a learner through the product.

**Key findings**:
- **School activation crossed the reachability line**: `app/admin/core-schools/new/page.tsx:52` → `POST /api/core/school` → `activateSchool()` (`lib/core/schoolActivation.ts:407`) now creates a school with its full academic-year/terms/classes/settings structure in one request — a genuine, UI-reachable improvement over Sprint 9A's 6-call manual sequence.
- **The same admin form discards the activation result**: `app/admin/core-schools/new/page.tsx` reads `data.error` on failure but never reads the `data.activation` the route now returns on success, immediately calling `router.push('/admin')` — a partial-activation failure is currently invisible in the UI.
- **Teacher invitation is a fresh instance of the "real API, no UI" pattern**: `app/api/core/teachers/route.ts` (new, `action: 'invite'|'accept'`, backed by `lib/core/teacherOnboarding.ts`) has zero `.tsx` callers anywhere in the repository.
- **Learner onboarding, same pattern**: `onboardLearner()` (`lib/core/learnerOnboarding.ts`), wired into `POST /api/core/learners` via a payload-shape branch, has zero `.tsx` callers.
- **A brand-new decision-support report was born with zero consumers**: `getSchoolAcademicReadiness()` (`lib/core/academicActivation.ts:223`) rolls up year/term/subjects/teachers/learners into one readiness verdict — genuinely useful, called from nowhere but its own test file.
- **Promotions and transfers remain confirmed dead ends**, explicitly scoped out of the new orchestration modules rather than closed.
- **The operational event bus remains a confirmed silent no-op** — zero `publishEvent()` calls exist anywhere in the new/changed files; a fuller cron inventory this sprint found 10 of 18 cron jobs scheduled nowhere (no `vercel.json` entry, no GitHub Actions workflow entry).
- **Attendance still does not exist as a system**; `core-term` still has no nav link; `buildPrincipalDashboard` remains fully orphaned; no role model exists above teacher/parent/student at the auth layer; 17 of 21 named school roles (Dean of Studies, Admissions, Examinations Officer, Librarian, etc.) have zero code-level evidence anywhere in the repository.

**Architectural documents referenced**: builds directly on `docs/architecture/sprint-10b-school-operations-audit.md` and `docs/architecture/sprint-9a-phase2-school-activation-audit.md` (this sprint's evidence baseline, re-verified rather than assumed); the Sprint 9B–9G implementation-log entries above (the orchestration work whose reachability this audit checked).

**ADR**: None — audit only, no canonical conflict found, no architecture change proposed. The gaps found are reachability gaps (no UI consumer), the same class Sprint 10B already established does not trigger an ADR.

**Tests added**: None (read-only, no code changed).

**Rollback considerations**: None — no code, schema, or data was touched; the only artifacts are this log entry and the audit document itself.

## 2026-07-16 — Sprint 10A (partial): End-of-Term Identity Bridge Repair

**What changed**: fixed two confirmed, live FK-space collisions in `computeTermSummaries` (`lib/core/assessments.ts`), the term-aggregation step of `runEndOfTerm()` (`lib/core/endOfTerm.ts`). Discovered while auditing the End-of-Term pipeline for reachability per the Sprint 10A mission brief; scope was deliberately narrowed to this one confirmed blocker rather than the mission's full 9-step scope, per user direction.

**Root cause (two bugs, same underlying cause)**: `computeTermSummaries` mixes two id spaces that a single `classId`/`studentId` cannot both satisfy, confirmed live via `information_schema`:
1. `learner_marks.student_id` FKs to legacy `students.id` (Sprint 9F's academic bridge writes it via `saveScores`), but `term_subject_summaries.learner_id` FKs to Core `learners.id` — the function was writing the legacy id straight into the Core FK column.
2. `class_assessments.class_id` FKs to legacy `teacher_classes.id`, but `term_subject_summaries.class_id` FKs to Core `classes.id` — the function used one `classId` parameter to both read `class_assessments` (needs legacy) and write `term_subject_summaries` (needs Core), which no single id can do.

For any Core-native/bridged school, every `upsertTermSubjectSummaries` call inside `computeTermSummaries` was therefore either finding zero assessments (Core classId passed in, matching nothing in the legacy-keyed `class_assessments`) or failing its FK check outright (legacy classId/studentId passed in, violating `term_subject_summaries`' Core-keyed FKs) — breaking the Assessment → Evidence → Projection → Summaries chain End-of-Term depends on, before Report Cards, Ranking, or Publication are ever reached.

**Repair**: reused Sprint 9F's existing `external_id` bridge mechanism in both directions, no new identity or schema:
- `lib/repositories/teacher.repository.ts`: added `findExternalIdsByStudentIds` (batched legacy `students.id` → Core `learners.id`, via `students.external_id`) and `findLegacyClassIdsByExternalId` (Core `classes.id` → legacy `teacher_classes.id`(s), via `teacher_classes.external_id`).
- `lib/repositories/assessment.repository.ts`: added `findPublishedAssessmentsByClassIds` (batched `.in()` variant of the existing single-class lookup, since a Core class can bridge to zero or more legacy classes).
- `lib/core/assessments.ts::computeTermSummaries`: now resolves the Core `classId` to its bridged legacy class id(s) before reading `class_assessments`/`learner_marks`, and resolves each mark's `student_id` back to its Core learner before writing `term_subject_summaries.learner_id`. A mark with no resolvable bridge is skipped (not written with an invalid id) — matches the function's existing "skip unknown subject code" defensive pattern.

**Exit Question answer** (full reasoning in the audit doc): a school running EduNexus tomorrow would get real value from the teacher dashboard, legacy assessment/evidence/parent-notification loop, and parent report/Compass/Career views — and would leave the product entirely for attendance, all headteacher decisions, all academic-office administration, and anything meant to route automatically through the event bus. The bridge from "LMS on steroids" to "Educational Operating System" is not more intelligence — it is reachable doors to capability that in several cases (the principal dashboard, promotions, transfers, this sprint's own End-of-Term UI) already exists in code today.

**Architectural documents referenced**: builds directly on Sprint 10A Commits 1-3 (`docs/engineering/implementation-log.md`'s own three prior entries) and the Sprint 6-9 operating-system blueprint series in `docs/architecture/` — this audit is the first pass to check that blueprint against actually-reachable UI rather than backend capability alone.

**ADR**: None — audit only, no canonical conflict found, no architecture change proposed.

**Tests added**: None (read-only, no code changed).

**Rollback considerations**: None — no code, schema, or data was touched; the only artifact is the audit document itself.

---

## 2026-07-16 — Sprint 10B: School Operations Layer Audit (read-only)

**What changed**: nothing — this was a read-only architectural audit, no code/schema/migration touched. Full findings in `docs/architecture/sprint-10b-school-operations-audit.md`. Method: three parallel research passes (daily timeline/teacher/headteacher workspaces; academic office/parent/student experience; dashboards/events inventory), each gathering file-cited evidence, followed by synthesis and the Part 9/10/Exit-Question judgment calls done directly, not delegated.

**Headline finding**: the audit found no missing engine — Assessment, Evidence, Projection, Ranking, Grading, and Compass are real and untouched — but found a reachability gap between real backend capability and any UI a school staff member would find, repeated at nearly every layer, plus one platform-wide plumbing failure (the operational event bus) that is a complete, silent no-op in production.

**Key findings**:
- **No role model above teacher/parent/student at the auth layer** (`lib/auth/getRole.ts:5`, `proxy.ts`): Headteacher, Academic Office, Class Teacher, and Subject Teacher all land on the identical `/teacher/dashboard`. A separate `SchoolUserRole` (school_admin/headteacher/deputy_headteacher) exists only inside Core's per-school membership and is never consulted by routing.
- **Sprint 10A's own new UI is unreachable**: `app/teacher/core-term/{page,status}.tsx` work correctly (verified live, Commits 1-3) but have zero inbound links from any nav or dashboard — reachable only by typing the URL.
- **Attendance does not exist as a system** — no table, despite being marketed on the public site as a live capability.
- **A Core-published report card notifies no one**: the only automatic parent-notification path (`lib/academicClinic/assessmentPipeline.ts`) is wired to the legacy schema only; the new Core publish flow built this session doesn't call it.
- **Academic Office operations are entirely API-only**: years, terms, subjects, promotions, and transfers each have a real, working route and zero UI — confirmed dead ends (sole importer of `lib/core/promotions.ts`/`lib/core/transfers.ts` is their own API route).
- **A real, sophisticated headteacher-dashboard backend exists and has zero frontend**: `buildPrincipalDashboard()`, served at `app/api/school/intelligence/route.ts`, fully built, correctly authorized, never called from any page anywhere in the repository.
- **The entire platform event bus is a confirmed no-op in production**: `platform_events`/`event_subscriptions`/`event_deliveries` (migration `20260701_phase8_platform_foundation.sql`) never appear in `lib/database.types.ts` (generated from the live schema) — the migration was never applied live. All 27 `publishEvent()` call sites (23 distinct event types, including this session's own `teacher.assessment.published`/`teacher.report_card.published`) fail, are caught, and are only logged. Even if the table existed, nothing would consume the events: no subscription is ever created, no internal handler is ever registered, and the one dispatch cron that exists isn't listed in `vercel.json`.
- **`/admin/*` is hardcoded to one personal email** (`proxy.ts:98-103`) — there is no platform-admin role model at all.

**School readiness verdict** (Part 9): ready for one to a handful of teachers on the legacy path; the new Core End-of-Term workflow is technically ready but practically undiscoverable; not yet ready for a 20-teacher/500-learner school's administrative operations (no academic-office UI, no attendance) or multi-campus/multi-school operation (no cross-school visibility, no real admin role model, broken event bus).

**"LMS on steroids" classification** (Part 10): Evidence/Projection, Compass, and Career Intelligence genuinely reach Educational Intelligence tier. Academic Office operations, attendance, and notifications currently sit *below* commodity-LMS baseline (an API with no screen is a regression from a plain SIS admin panel, not an enhancement). The Operating System tier the sprint framing aims at is not yet reached — the intelligence is real, the operational doors around it mostly aren't.

**Exit Question answer** (full reasoning in the audit doc): a school running EduNexus tomorrow would get real value from the teacher dashboard, legacy assessment/evidence/parent-notification loop, and parent report/Compass/Career views — and would leave the product entirely for attendance, all headteacher decisions, all academic-office administration, and anything meant to route automatically through the event bus. The bridge from "LMS on steroids" to "Educational Operating System" is not more intelligence — it is reachable doors to capability that in several cases (the principal dashboard, promotions, transfers, this sprint's own End-of-Term UI) already exists in code today.

**Architectural documents referenced**: builds directly on Sprint 10A Commits 1-3 (`docs/engineering/implementation-log.md`'s own three prior entries) and the Sprint 6-9 operating-system blueprint series in `docs/architecture/` — this audit is the first pass to check that blueprint against actually-reachable UI rather than backend capability alone.

**ADR**: None — audit only, no canonical conflict found, no architecture change proposed.

**Tests added**: None (read-only, no code changed).

**Rollback considerations**: None — no code, schema, or data was touched; the only artifact is the audit document itself.
